# Current Smart Money Research Snapshot — 2026-11-23 (Phase 580)

**Audit cutoff:** 2026-11-23 18:15 UTC. **Main HEAD after merge:** `db463ddba9176f9482d3f424e39a66b5eaafdfcd`. Full report: [Phase 580 — FINRA ATS tier-specific clock and entry-date identity guard](research/PHASE580_FINRA_ATS_TIER_AND_IDENTITY_ENTRY_GUARD_2026-11-23.md). PR [#209](https://github.com/jafelix81/NuevaEstrategia/pull/209) merged by squash.

- **Repository / validation:** 376 branches; 72 open PRs after merge. Phase 580 PR workflow [45236294572](https://github.com/jafelix81/NuevaEstrategia/actions/runs/45236294572) and Phase 579 regression workflow [45236295142](https://github.com/jafelix81/NuevaEstrategia/actions/runs/45236295142) both completed successfully. Each ran **25/25 synthetic tests**. No artifacts expected; both workflows are offline-only.
- **Phase 580 correction:** the 21/22-calendar-day publication clock is now explicitly restricted to a common, present `tierIdentifier=T1`. Missing, mixed, T2 and OTCE groups fail closed. This reflects FINRA API specification v0.4: T1 uses a two-week reporting delay; T2/OTC use four weeks. The prior gate was based on the Tier 1 census but did not enforce the tier field.
- **Identity correction:** effective-dated security-master records must cover both the report-week start and the first executable session, in addition to being known no later than that session's open, having certified record-time semantics, and uniquely identifying listing/security/issuer. A mapping expiring before entry no longer qualifies.
- **Data boundary:** no real FINRA rows, live API request, raw data, vendor identity sample or licensed data were used. No T2/OTCE clock was reconstructed. Historical identity, historical universe membership and complete active/inactive total-return/delisting coverage remain unresolved.

- **Actions / repository:** 8,076 total runs; latest 100 = 96 success / 4 failure; zero queued/in-progress. Branches: 374 (100 + 100 + 100 + 74); open PRs: 72. Latest Phase 577 main run [45204833774](https://github.com/jafelix81/NuevaEstrategia/actions/runs/45204833774) passed 16/16 synthetic tests; no artifacts expected.

Existing Issue #194 comment 7104521256 was updated in place; no dispatch/rerun/cron change.
- **New FINRA ATS field-semantic resolution:** official FINRA API spec v0.4 defines `lastReportedDate` as the last time a firm sent an update to underlying data, `lastUpdateDate` as the later of lastReportedDate or initialPublishedDate, and initialPublishedDate as the first Monday/next business day after the T1/T2/OTC reporting delay. Therefore lastReportedDate/lastUpdateDate are not verified public revision-release timestamps. Revised rows (lastReportedDate > initialPublishedDate) remain quarantined; stable rows can use initialPublishedDate plus the first NYSE regular session strictly after it.
- **Existing stable-panel evidence:** Phase 385 validated the date-level T1 publication clock across 209/209 partitions; Phase 375 separately found 432,804/438,798 symbol-week groups revision-stable (98.633996%) and 96.943175% of mapped aggregate share volume. These metrics have different grains and denominators. Phase 535's broader recent API panel spans 259 consecutive weeks (2021-11-15 to 2026-10-26), but exact effective-dated F80 security identity and historical universe membership remain unverified. Phase 385 PR #61 is Draft and diverged 189 ahead / 455 behind main; do not merge as-is.
- **Other source gates:** FINRA Short Interest settlement 2026-11-13 is scheduled for 2026-11-24; documented availability gate 21:40 UTC / 14:40 Hermosillo, subject to authorized credentials and non-commercial permitted use. SEC 13D subject-issuer panel remains quarantined pending SEC_USER_AGENT and identity/dissemination controls. Cboe/Massive/ThetaData/OCC options remain license/PIT/coverage-gated; MIDAS is exchange-market-structure context only; CFTC TFF remains prospectively under-sampled; 13F is parked and N-PORT raw share-growth is killed.

- **Weekly API:** Phase 100 sampled 10 partitions / 968,139 rows; all API Record-Total counts reconciled, 100% initialPublishedDate on symbol aggregates, observed 21/35-day T1/T2 publication lags, 1,882 later updates, two missing-symbol rows and two LND duplicate-key groups. ## Phase 459 — Form 4 joint-owner attribution and duplicate-event gate (2026-11-17)

- Current SEC Form 4 instructions (SEC 1474, 03-26, Instruction 4(b)(v)) permit joint/group filings to include both the same transaction reportable by multiple beneficial owners and securities owned separately by any joint/group filer. Therefore, reporting-owner count is not trade count or independent-buyer count.
- SEC Ownership XML spec v3 (2010) models repeated reportingOwner blocks separately from nonDerivativeTable/derivativeTable; it says there is no primary owner and signatures are not tied to reporting owners. The current Form 4 instructions remain consistent with joint/group filing. Current XML v5.5 (2026-03-18) is listed by SEC; its ZIP was not unpacked in this phase, so a v5.5 schema-level recheck remains required.
- Real SEC example: CIK 0001612720, 2018-06-11, accessions 0001140361-18-028469 and 0001140361-18-028465 explicitly identify two Forms 4 split for the same event because of the >10 reporting-person limit. Do not count accession count as independent trade count.
- Inherited Phase 458 endpoint cohorts: P-coded rows inside multi-owner accessions = 1,134/7,180 (15.794%) in 2012Q1 and 694/5,183 (13.390%) in 2026Q3. These two endpoints are not a full-history prevalence estimate.
- Killed bulk-only proxy FORM4_MULTI_OWNER_OWNER_COUNT_CLUSTER. Quarantine multi-owner lines unless explicit unique line-to-owner evidence exists; do not use owner order, signature order/count, roles or proximity. Candidate split filings require explicit same-event evidence before deduplication.
- Added fail-closed XML attribution helper, nine synthetic regressions, machine-readable summary and offline CI. No raw SEC data, vendor data or live SEC request. No forward-return, alpha, OOS, bootstrap, LOO or holdout test. F80_TOP20_PARTIAL50_BASELINE_C20 remains frozen.
- Report: [Phase 459](research/PHASE459_FORM4_JOINT_OWNER_ATTRIBUTION_FEASIBILITY_AUDIT_2026-11-17.md) · [summary](research/PHASE459_FORM4_JOINT_OWNER_ATTRIBUTION_SUMMARY_2026-11-17.json).

## Phase 457 — SEC Form 3/4/5 full-history coverage and PIT-clock audit (2026-11-17)

- SEC's official Insider Transactions Data Sets page currently covers Jan 2006–Sep 2026 and lists 83 quarterly ZIPs (2006Q1–2026Q3); sum of displayed archive sizes is approximately 887.43 MB compressed. Dataset is quarterly/as-filed and includes Forms 3/4/5 plus amendments; bulk archive posting is not the filing's first-public-receipt time.
- SEC data dictionary: up to eight UTF-8 TSV tables; SUBMISSION includes ACCESSION_NUMBER, date-only FILING_DATE, PERIOD_OF_REPORT, DATE_OF_ORIG_SUB, document type, issuer CIK/name/symbol; REPORTINGOWNER includes owner CIK/role; transaction tables include TRANS_DATE, TRANS_CODE, shares/price, acquired/disposed and direct/indirect ownership; FOOTNOTES and OWNER_SIGNATURE are required context.
- Critical semantics: transaction code P is “open market or private purchase,” not confirmed exchange execution. Forms 4/A are amendments, not automatically new trades. SEC added AFF10B5ONE to 2023–2025 datasets in July 2025 and says future sets will include it; absent historical flags are unknown, not false.
- EDGAR ACCEPTANCE-DATETIME is in the complete submission header; SEC says sec.gov availability is often 1–3 minutes later but is not guaranteed and no exact public-availability timestamp is provided. Use accession acceptance plus prospective first-seen poll receipt; never use transaction date, filing date or quarterly archive date as a tradable event timestamp. Forms 3/4/5 submitted after 17:30 and before 22:00 ET can still receive same-day filing date/dissemination.
- Phase 455's six-quarter sample remains descriptive only (413 candidate rows, 262 accessions, 109 issuer CIKs, 224 issuer-filing events; five exact current-335 symbol overlaps across VRTX/CRSP/NTLA/OPEN). No representative full-history or point-in-time universe coverage established.
- Operational preflight at 2026-11-17 14:28 UTC: latest overall run [44087732566](https://github.com/jafelix81/NuevaEstrategia/actions/runs/44087732566) passed 6 tests and uploaded aggregate artifact 14329718579; Phase 454 PR CI [44086525354](https://github.com/jafelix81/NuevaEstrategia/actions/runs/44086525354) passed offline tests with live capture skipped; latest main scheduled SEC run [44083941814](https://github.com/jafelix81/NuevaEstrategia/actions/runs/44083941814) failed before Phase 454 merge. The 13:47 UTC post-migration schedule slot was not observed by 14:28 UTC; workflow enabled state cannot be inspected through the current read interface. CFTC 22:25 UTC slot remains ahead. Actions total 7,696; zero active/queued; 289 branches; 56 open PRs; 71 workflow files.
- Next gates: confirm monitored-contact SEC_USER_AGENT; pilot official 2026Q3 archive; if valid, process all 83 archives sequentially with hashes and aggregate-only artifacts; join accession acceptance and prospective first-seen; finish effective-dated security-class/listing and historical F80 membership crosswalk. No full archive downloaded, no return/OOS/alpha test, no signal or production change. `F80_TOP20_PARTIAL50_BASELINE_C20` remains frozen.
- Report: [Phase 457](research/PHASE457_SEC_FORM345_FULL_HISTORY_PIT_AUDIT_2026-11-17.md) · [summary](research/PHASE457_SEC_FORM345_FULL_HISTORY_PIT_SUMMARY_2026-11-17.json).

## Phase 456 — Nasdaq investor activity and Options Pulse source-feasibility audit (2026-11-17)

- Main HEAD at preflight: `20544866495c1f9c5d36cd117c1498637489d2e8`. Latest overall run [44087732566](https://github.com/jafelix81/NuevaEstrategia/actions/runs/44087732566) passed; audit job uploaded aggregate-only artifact 14329718579. Phase 454 CI [44086525354](https://github.com/jafelix81/NuevaEstrategia/actions/runs/44086525354) passed offline regression; live capture was skipped on PR. Latest main scheduled SEC run [44083941814](https://github.com/jafelix81/NuevaEstrategia/actions/runs/44083941814) passed offline tests but failed live capture on the pre-migration ledger schema. Phase 454 is now merged; next configured SEC slot is 2026-11-17 13:47 UTC. CFTC next configured slot is 22:25 UTC. Explicit in-progress/queued queries returned zero. Branches 288 (100+100+88 unique), open PRs 56, workflow files 71. No workflow was dispatched, rerun or duplicated.
- New source family audited from official Nasdaq documentation: UREF/DF daily retail flow (2016-01, 10,000+ tickers, buy/sell volume and VWAP, previous-day delivery by 10:00 UTC, PIT tickers, incomplete pre-2021 dates, <$1 exclusion); UREFINT 15-minute retail flow (2016-01, <30-minute stated lag, but dedicated and combined documentation expose inconsistent schemas); NOSI/Options Pulse (2019-01, 10-minute market/sector/symbol sentiment derived from customer and professional-customer opening buys, proprietary score and FIGI mapping); USRFH (2021-01, user-level retail trades/holdings with deliberately scaled/noisy volume and price, holdings monthly before 2024-10-29); and Nasdaq Trade Outline NOTO/PHOTO (venue-specific electronic option trades with participant-origin and opening/closing buy/sell fields, NOTO history 2011-11, PHOTO 2009-01).
- Disposition: NOTO/PHOTO is the highest-priority conditional Nasdaq options candidate because its open/close and participant-origin fields are more transparent than NOSI, but licensing, venue coverage, sample bytes and first-receipt timestamps remain gates. UREF/DF is a medium-priority retail crowding/contrarian control, not smart money; 335-name coverage and historical first receipt are unverified. UREFINT is conditional and lower priority until its schema conflict is resolved. NOSI is a low-priority comparator only. USRFH is low-priority retail breadth/crowding research; noisy absolute volume/price and monthly pre-2024-10-29 holdings limit its use. Free RTAT10 is top-ten activity-selected only, not a 335-name cross-sectional source.
- Nasdaq's Options Pulse whitepaper reports 1,548 observations from 2019-01 to 2025-02; Granger p-values are 0.7299 (1-day), 0.0001 (2-day), 0.0003 (3-day). The vendor's moving-average/next-day-entry/holding-x+1 portfolio description omits sufficient details for independent replication of thresholds, trade count, turnover, costs, risk, multiple testing and untouched OOS. Treat as a hypothesis, not alpha evidence.
- All candidate data remain premium/proprietary or otherwise gated. No vendor account, trial, purchase, API key, API request, raw data download or vendor contact was made. No signal, alpha, forward-return/OOS, bootstrap, LOO or holdout test ran. No production changes. `F80_TOP20_PARTIAL50_BASELINE_C20`, universe, parameters, capital, exposure, holding rules and holdout remain frozen.
- Report: [Phase 456](research/PHASE456_NASDAQ_INVESTOR_ACTIVITY_AND_OPTIONS_PULSE_FEASIBILITY_AUDIT_2026-11-17.md) · [summary](research/PHASE456_NASDAQ_INVESTOR_ACTIVITY_FEASIBILITY_SUMMARY_2026-11-17.json).

## Phase 454 — SEC Form 144 event-ledger schema migration (2026-11-17)

- Real scheduled run [44083941814](https://github.com/jafelix81/NuevaEstrategia/actions/runs/44083941814) reached live capture after the :47 re-arm. Offline tests passed; live capture failed in acceptance reconciliation with `ValueError: invalid literal for int() with base 10: '2026-11-16T16:55:39-05:00'`. No artifact or append commit; runner-workspace changes did not persist.
- Root cause: Phase 444 added `cross_feed_provenance_complete` and `capture_poll_status` to current `EVENT_FIELDS`, but committed `events.csv` retained the old header. The append routine wrote new-order rows without migrating the header, shifting subsequent values two columns; `feed_path_cik` then contained the raw feed-updated timestamp.
- Phase 454 adds an idempotent, atomic, fail-closed schema migration before the Phase 403 live capture. Existing values are preserved by column name; new fields remain blank for historical rows. Unknown/duplicate headers and extra row columns stop the migration rather than dropping data. The existing Phase 403 workflow runs the migration tests and includes a migration summary in its artifact.
- Report: [Phase 454 audit](research/PHASE454_FORM144_EVENT_LEDGER_SCHEMA_MIGRATION_2026-11-17.md) · [summary](research/PHASE454_FORM144_EVENT_LEDGER_SCHEMA_MIGRATION_SUMMARY_2026-11-17.json).

Live capture was correctly skipped on PR CI; next scheduled main capture is the production validation gate.
- No manual replay, no fabricated first-seen time, no signal admission, no forward-return test. `F80_TOP20_PARTIAL50_BASELINE_C20` remains frozen.

## Phase 453 — SEC Form 345 as-filed issuer-symbol and security-class crosswalk audit (2026-11-17)

- Main HEAD at preflight: `cfe97c971cceb027a50f924ce9984ba6489492d5` (Phase 452 state update). Re-audited immutable SEC Form 345 artifact [12781850078](https://github.com/jafelix81/NuevaEstrategia/actions/runs/40606706518/artifacts/12781850078), outer SHA-256 `203d7b1d2e29c999da318456af72e621cb30ef9a470d8f1610d088f9bebecb66`; 2012Q1 and 2026Q3 nested ZIP hashes verified.
- Both actual quarterly SUBMISSION.tsv archives contain as-filed `ISSUERTRADINGSYMBOL`; the field is issuer/accession-level, not transaction-row/security-level. Form 4/4-A symbol field was nonblank in 63,678/63,678 2012Q1 submissions and 34,148/34,148 2026Q3 submissions, but sentinel values were 492 (0.773%) and 429 (1.256%). Transaction-level SECURITY_TITLE is available separately.
- Class ambiguity is real: 51 (2012Q1) and 85 (2026Q3) accessions contain multiple common class/series tokens; 48 and 84 respectively have only one issuer-symbol string. The symbol field alone therefore cannot identify every class-specific transaction. Raw collisions, comma lists, exchange prefixes, numeric values and multiple symbols per issuer-quarter are preserved as diagnostics, not silently normalized.
- Decision: use the bulk dataset's as-filed issuer symbol as the primary issuer-level anchor; retain effective-dated class/listing crosswalk and unresolved-state gates. Phase 439's 53/96 legacy-HTML figure is not a ceiling on bulk-field availability, but neither archive endpoint proves full F80 identity coverage. No return/alpha/OOS test or signal admission.

## Phase 442 — Norgate terminal-return, Python API and license gate recheck

- New finding: Norgate explicitly does not provide delisting returns or terminal recovery values; a final available bar is an approximation, not observed terminal proceeds. It cannot alone supply strict total-economic-return labels through delistings.
- Confirmed the documented Python assetid API and explicit adjustment/padding controls, but prior ticker history, CUSIP/ISIN and record-known correction vintages remain absent.
- The current EULA (20261107) permits private cloud processing only for the individual's personal non-commercial use; no shared/public data access, business/client use, or post-expiry retention of extracted content. Current Platinum pricing is USD 346.50/6 months or USD 630/12 months.
- Decision: keep Norgate as a conditional price/identity cross-check only. Prefer CRSP's PERMNO/PERMCO and delisting-return fields for terminal outcomes if licensed access exists. No return/alpha/OOS test or portfolio change.

## Phase 429 — ThetaData v3 access, extraction and license reconciliation (2026-11-16)

- Fresh official-doc review extends Phase 391. Options subscription matrix: FREE EOD from 2023-06-01; VALUE 1m from 2020-01-01; STANDARD tick from 2016-01-01; PRO tick from 2012-06-01. The detailed matrix and public pricing pages are inconsistent: retail Options Pro advertises 12 years while the detailed tier table starts 2012-06-01 and the commercial page advertises 14 years. Require written endpoint-by-endpoint history confirmation before any purchase.
- Historical option/history/trade_quote pairs OPRA trades with NBBO; set exclusive=true so quote_timestamp < trade_timestamp. option/list/contracts can list contracts traded/quoted on a specified historical date, a possible PIT optionability census. Neither establishes vendor first receipt, corrections/bust vintages, or full 335-name/inactive coverage.
- Extraction constraints are material: no market-wide historical wildcard; per-underlying requests, expiration=* for that underlying, multi-day maximum one month; recommended options tick chunks are one week and one day for very liquid names, keeping responses under ~1m ticks. Pro is 8 concurrent requests account-wide; requests may queue and a full queue can return 429. Historical flat files are only the most recent 7 calendar days (option trade_quote ~1.2GB/day), so they do not solve deep bulk backfill.
- OPRA OI normally arrives ~06:30 ET and represents the previous trading day's OI; absent messages are missing, not zero. Vendor's underlying equity history is UTP-dependent before 2020 and CTA-only examples (SPY, GE) lack older stock/Greek history; raw option flow may still exist, but historical Greek/delta weighting requires a separate validated underlying source.
- License gate: commercial Options Data listed at $1,600/month billed annually (startup discount advertised from $500/month); retail Options Pro at $160/month is individual/personal-use only. No account, trial, key, sample, contact or purchase initiated.
- Decision: retain ThetaData as a strong **conditional raw-OPRA microstructure** candidate, not a verified institutional-flow source. No sample bytes, PIT receipt/correction ledger, full-universe coverage, alpha/OOS or holdout test. No source/signal admitted; F80 and production unchanged. Full report: research/PHASE429_THETADATA_V3_ACCESS_EXTRACTION_AND_LICENSE_AUDIT_2026-11-16.md.

- **Phase 425 — Form 144 timing and non-execution semantics:** current SEC Corporation Finance CFI page is last updated 2026-10-15; Question 136.05 was revised 2026-09-25. It states no waiting period is required between broker-order placement and execution if the Form 144 is transmitted concurrently to both the Commission and principal exchange. This means the SEC filing cannot be assumed to lead execution; the repository does not capture exchange-side receipt/dissemination. CFI 136.01 also says no amendment is required if the proposed securities are not sold, so absence of a 144/A cannot classify execution, cancellation or outstanding status. Keep Form 144 as a selective affiliate supply/order-placement hypothesis, not a confirmed sale or “smart-money” signal. Preserve original accession as event root and every 144/A as an immutable version; no backdating or amendment double-counting. Existing Phase 398 counts (87/193 matched amendments changed at least one audited tranche field) reinforce version-state treatment. Report: `research/PHASE425_FORM144_ORDER_TIMING_AND_NONEXECUTION_SEMANTICS_2026-11-16.md`. No code or workflow changed; no return test or signal admission.

- **Phase 420 — correction to Phase 419:** the Binomial(n, 11/53) event-count approximation incorrectly assumes independent high-state indicators. Phase 387's percentile is a 52-observation rolling rank; overlapping windows create negative dependence even under IID continuous raw inputs. A 200,000-path synthetic rolling-rank simulation (seed 20261115; no CFTC data) gives a 91.11% joint probability for 116 complete-case training observations plus 166 consecutive evaluation observations, and 95.33% for 120 + 170. The first 52 reports are feature-reference warm-up; approximately four weekly intervals are added for training-outcome maturity and four for final evaluation-outcome maturity. Conditional weekly calendar envelopes are therefore 342 reports (~6.6 years) for the ~90% joint plan and 350 (~6.7 years) for the ~95% plan. Phase 419's 301/319 estimates are superseded. These are synthetic event-count planning diagnostics, not power analysis or empirical TFF frequencies.
- Phase 387's features, 0.80 threshold, 20/40 training and 30/100 evaluation event gates, 20-session outcomes, chronological rules, block bootstrap/HAC and kill rules remain unchanged. The first 52 warm-up reports have no valid percentile of their own and are not counted as complete-case high/non-high rows for paired baseline/augmented training.

## Phase 414 — FINRA price-provider candidate audit (2026-11-15)

- Added `research/PHASE414_FINRA_PRICE_PROVIDER_CANDIDATE_AUDIT_2026-11-15.md`. Massive Stocks Advanced is the primary conditional candidate because official documentation exposes listed US daily bars back to 2003-09-10 and effective-dated active/inactive ticker lists. Its security-master and corporate-action limitations are material: no vendor-as-known historical vintages, no permanent identifier across every security change, older identifiers/type/exchange fields can be blank, and daily bars are not a complete total-return series without explicit dividend treatment.
- Massive pricing is $199/month for individual use only. Norgate Platinum lists $630/year, daily history back to 1990, selected delisted securities and historical index constituents, but uses a local Windows workflow and its trial is limited to two years. EODHD lists $19.99/month personal EOD access and delisted EOD histories, but pre-2018 delisted names are documented as EOD-only. Tiingo's pricing page does not establish delisted completeness.
- Decision: no provider admitted until all 335 diagnostic symbols, seven missing names, delisted/predecessor identities, >=220 daily observations per required symbol, OHLC corporate-action adjustment and independent price concordance pass. No subscription, key, vendor contact or data request. No return test.

## Phase 398 — Form 144/A amendment lineage and tranche-level security resolution (2026-11-14)

Overlapping counts: proposed units 44, proposed value 59, outstanding denominator 19, approximate sale date 18, broker 20, exchange 12, tranche count 3, security-class set 6. Four amendment chains reach a second amendment level in-panel. Form 144/A must be stored as a version of the original proposed-sale order, with each version known only from its own filing date; never count it as an independent new sale or backdate revised quantities/values.

The single ASTS case has XML issuer CIK 0001780312 and filer/seller CIK 0001053507; issuer-side CIK resolves the subject to ASTS, subject to the effective-dated crosswalk. The SEC 2023/2025 Zillow 10-Ks and ASTS 2023 10-K predate the relevant observations and document these ticker/class mappings.
- Current eCFR 17 CFR 230.144(h)(3) still requires Form 144 concurrent with broker-order placement or direct market-maker execution. SEC Release 33-11159 extends EDGAR filing hours to 10 p.m.; it does not replace the concurrent requirement with a two-business-day deadline. Keep the conservative next-regular-session-after-filing-date daily clock; no accepted timestamp or first-public-receipt ledger exists in the Phase 67 panel.

## Phase 392 — FINRA ATS scope reconciliation and OptionMetrics TradeFlow lineage (2026-11-14)

- **Phase 385 latest rerun is complete, not in progress.** Run [43536490386](https://github.com/jafelix81/NuevaEstrategia/actions/runs/43536490386), job 132311996238, completed successfully at 2026-11-14 05:40:14 UTC. It retrieved 209/209 T1 partitions, 10,917,933 full T1 rows across 2,292 pages, and 438,822 raw `ATS_W_SMBL` aggregate rows. All 209 partitions had one valid `initialPublishedDate`, all matched the documented 21/22-day NYSE calendar (186 at 21 days; 23 at 22 days), and each mapped to the first NYSE regular session strictly after publication. Artifact [14100393795](https://github.com/jafelix81/NuevaEstrategia/actions/runs/43536490386/artifacts/14100393795), ZIP SHA-256 `3630ce815040a2487ebfc86369552f21da3ee1b58e235f169cd0f604cffc3a46`. The earlier run 43536354917 also passed. As of 2026-11-14 06:08 UTC, no Actions runs are queued or in progress.
- **PIT boundary remains explicit:** Phase 385 sets `intraday_first_receipt_certified=false`, `effective_dated_security_master_gate_passed=false`, and `historical_return_test_authorized=false`. This is a conservative date-level availability bound, not an exact receipt ledger and not an alpha admission.
- **Count-grain reconciliation:** Phase 385 counts raw `ATS_W_SMBL` product-grain rows (438,822; 432,828 with `lastUpdateDate == initialPublishedDate`; 5,994 updated later). Phase 375 counts unioned symbol-week groups after reconciling distinct CTS/UTP product rows (438,798 groups; 432,804 stable; 5,994 revised). The 24-row difference is consistent with the distinct grains and Phase 374's product-type consolidation logic; no row-level crosswalk was retained. Keep these denominators separate and do not describe them as a failed reconciliation.
- **FINRA semantic correction:** FINRA Rule 4552 requires each ATS to report its weekly equity volume/trade count and use a unique MPID; FINRA publishes the reported volume for each security for each ATS and week. Therefore the 38 MPID/CRD-derived keys in Phase 375 are ATS/firm reporting entities (venues), not customers, traders, beneficial owners or verified institutions. The HHI/top-share measures are **off-exchange ATS venue concentration**, not institutional-investor or “smart money” concentration. Reclassify FINRA T1 ATS as a market-structure/venue-concentration candidate. It may be investigated for liquidity, venue fragmentation or concentration hypotheses only after security identity, historical-universe and price/delisting gates pass. Official sources: FINRA [Rule 4552 overview in the OTC Transparency User Guide](https://www.finra.org/sites/default/files/OTC-transparency-website-user-guide.pdf) and [Weekly Summary API](https://developer.finra.org/docs/api-explorer/query_api-equity-weekly_summary).
- **OptionMetrics product lineage is now confirmed at the product-family level:** the official TradeFlow page states that TradeFlow is an evolution of IvyDB Signed Volume. The September 28, 2026 methodology paper documents four relational tables (`Signed_Volume`, `Trade_Conditions`, `Market_Structure`, `Signed_Exchange`) keyed by option symbol, security identifier and date. The new paper confirms five proprietary flow buckets (Retail 1–3; Institutional 1–2); Institutional 1 is contained within Institutional 2 and must not be added to it. Institutional 2 is specifically aggressive liquidity demand at/through the quote and excludes passive execution, midpoint prints, patient position-building and market-making. These are execution-style proxies, not beneficial-owner identities or total institutional participation. The paper's worked example is one SPY/FOMC session, not an independent accuracy or predictive validation.
- **Remaining TradeFlow gates:** current product lineage is clear, but exact field-level backward compatibility with the repository's earlier four-table schema, methodology/version vintages, historical first-delivery/correction ledger, business-use license, broad F80/inactive-underlying coverage and sample reconciliation remain unproven. The advertised January 2016 history is backfill; it is not proof the current TradeFlow classifier or fields were available in 2016. Treat pre-prospective history as retrospective research only unless vendor vintages establish otherwise. No vendor contact, trial, account, sample acquisition or purchase occurred. Official sources: [TradeFlow product page](https://optionmetrics.com/ivydb-tradeflow/), [TradeFlow methodology paper](https://optionmetrics.com/research/g-desimone-a-gupta-ivydb-tradeflow-a-framework-for-options-volume-classification-and-microstructure-analytics/), [TradeFlow brochure](https://optionmetrics.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/TradeFlow-Brochure-1-1.pdf).
- No return, alpha, OOS, bootstrap, LOO or holdout test was run. No signal is admitted; F80, production, universe, capital, exposure and parameters remain unchanged. See [Phase 392 report](research/PHASE392_FINRA_SCOPE_AND_OPTIONMETRICS_TRADEFLOW_LINEAGE_2026-11-14.md).

## Phase 391 — ThetaData / OPRA options PIT feasibility (2026-11-14)

- Official ThetaData v3 subscriptions now document options tick history from 2012-06-01 at Pro (2016-01-01 Standard), OPRA trade_quote, quote, OI, IV and trade Greeks. trade_quote can pair each OPRA trade with a quote timestamp strictly earlier than the trade via exclusive=true; requests are bounded to one month and require an expiration. This is a strong independent raw-OPRA reconstruction candidate, but no sample, F80 coverage, inactive/delisted coverage, contract-adjustment, correction-vintage or first-receipt audit has been run.
- ThetaData OI is normally reported around 06:30 ET and represents the previous trading day's close. Its generated EOD report is created at 17:15 ET; created is a generation timestamp, not a first-receipt ledger. No historical file may be treated as strict PIT without verified first receipt/correction vintages.
- Retail Pro is listed at $160/month for individual personal use only; commercial use is separate. No account, key, trial, contact or purchase occurred.
- OptionMetrics IvyDB Signed Volume's current official page confirms buyer-/seller-initiated daily volume, 5m/30m snapshots and EOD, with advertised history for every option since 2016. It remains the primary conditional signed-flow candidate, but the exact relationship to the repository's four-table TradeFlow schema must be verified; 2016 backfill is not proof of contemporaneous availability.
- Cboe Open-Close covers BZX/C1/C2/EDGX, not all OPRA; it provides capacity × buy/sell × open/close and has venue/date-specific OI and volume convention breaks. Keep it complementary, not a substitute for consolidated raw flow. Databento OPRA remains a shorter-history independent timestamp/coverage check from 2023.
- Decision: no options source is PIT-, universe- or alpha-admitted. See [Phase 391 report](research/PHASE391_THETADATA_OPRA_OPTIONS_PIT_FEASIBILITY_2026-11-14.md). No forward-return test, OOS, bootstrap, LOO or holdout use. F80 remains frozen.


## Phase 390 — FINRA security-master/public-source feasibility (2026-11-14)

- FINRA T1's 209-week date-level publication clock and Phase 375 revision-stable panel are necessary but not sufficient for returns research: the weekly symbol field is not a permanent listing/security ID.
- SEC's public ticker/CIK/exchange files are periodically updated EDGAR search associations, with no guarantee of accuracy/scope and no historical record-vintage clock.
- Massive All Tickers is a newly ranked secondary effective-date cross-check (historical-date endpoint from 2003-09-10, active/inactive records), but Massive explicitly says the historical list is not a copy of the record as known then; old records can have later last_updated_utc, identifiers are incomplete/variable, and free-plan reference history is only two years. It fails strict record-known PIT.
- Databento remains the best-documented two-clock candidate (ts_record / ts_effective, listing/security/issuer IDs); CRSP remains the preferred active/inactive return/delisting benchmark; Norgate remains a separate index-defined universe candidate. No vendor sample, contact, purchase or API-key use occurred.
- Decision: no ticker-only FINRA joins and no historical FINRA return test until permanent/effective-dated identity, historical eligibility, inactive prices and terminal/delisting returns pass. See [Phase 390 report](research/PHASE390_FINRA_PUBLIC_SECURITY_MASTER_FEASIBILITY_2026-11-14.md).

## Phase 31 — FINRA short-flow × short-interest divergence
- Status: KILLED as independent alpha.

- Result: horizon-dependent sign reversal; economic spreads' bootstrap intervals included zero. Do not tune horizons to rescue.

## Phase 21C — SEC Form 4 insider transactions
- Status: KILLED as a standalone signal in the current frozen-universe diagnostic.
- Run 39369692938 completed successfully with 24 training signal dates and 6 untouched final OOS dates (2025-02-25 through 2026-09-29).
- Official SEC dataset coverage is strong from 2016Q1 through 2026Q3; 301/335 universe CIKs mapped in the current implementation. KnowledgeDate uses filing date and downstream requires KnowledgeDate < SignalDate; same-day filings are excluded.
- Training-selected features were BUY_DAYS20 (H5), BUY_EVENTS60 (H10), and BUY_ADTV20 (H20). Final OOS mean top-minus-bottom was -0.12% (H5), +0.65% (H10), and -0.46% (H20); all bootstrap 95% CIs included zero. Residualized Spearman means were negative at all three horizons (-6.33%, -2.54%, -2.99%).
- Decision: do not tune insider features/horizons to rescue this implementation. The dataset remains reusable for other hypotheses only if a materially different, pre-registered mechanism is justified.

- Status: KILLED in the tested dose-response form.

- Top-minus-bottom decile returns were -11.77%, -5.56%, +12.03%, +6.12%, -4.78%, and -1.74% across the six periods. The sign is not stable and the sequence does not establish a monotonic institutional-flow effect.

FOXA control 0000950157-25-000788 is archived under subject CIK 0001754301 while the accession prefix is a different submitting account. ## 2026-11-04 superseding correction — Phase 87 filer/subject identity audit

- **Phase 35 run 41878429289 / artifact 13363886486 is rejected as a subject-issuer event panel.** Its code queries `data.sec.gov/submissions/CIK{cik}.json` for each target ticker CIK and assigns that queried CIK to `issuer_cik`; it does not consistently resolve the structured 13D/13G `issuerCik` / SEC header `SUBJECT COMPANY` role. The 20,266 rows are a CIK-submissions/filer-side extraction diagnostic, not 20,266 validated subject-issuer events.
- Confirmed SEC positive control: artifact row accession `0000895421-24-000178` was labelled `MS` / CIK 895421. The SEC XML for linked accession `0000895421-25-000185` identifies `previousAccessionNumber=0000895421-24-000178`, subject issuer CIK 1793855 (abrdn Global Infrastructure Income Fund), and reporting persons Morgan Stanley / Morgan Stanley Smith Barney LLC. It also says the prior 2024 filing misstated its event date. See [official SEC XML](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/895421/000089542125000185/primary_doc.xml).

## 2026-11-04 — Phase 88 CFTC TFF feasibility and source triage

- Phase 88 screens CFTC Traders in Financial Futures (TFF) as a **broad equity-index regime** input, not stock-level alpha. Official archives include S&P 500 and Nasdaq-100 contracts and public weekly position data by Dealer/Intermediary, Asset Manager/Institutional, Leveraged Funds, and Other Reportables.
- Historical data are available back to 2006, but early classifications are backcast. The CFTC explicitly states that historical publication dates are not centrally available beyond the latest 13 months. The 2025 funding lapse caused a report dated 2025-09-30, originally due 2025-10-03, to be published 2025-11-19. Therefore a standard Friday-after-Tuesday PIT rule is invalid in that period.
- Decision: retain TFF only as a secondary macro-regime candidate. Strict PIT gate fails until a reproducible historical publication calendar is reconstructed; no return test is authorized. See [Phase 88 feasibility audit](https://github.com/jafelix81/NuevaEstrategia/blob/phase88-cftc-tff-index-positioning-feasibility/research/PHASE88_CFTC_TFF_EQUITY_INDEX_POSITIONING_FEASIBILITY_2026-11-04.md).
- Other public microstructure candidates remain gated: SEC FTD has broad raw coverage but Phase 45 could not prove historical active-universe identity; SEC MIDAS has long daily coverage but no reliable per-quarter historical release timestamps; Cboe market-center volume is aggregate by market center and is suitable only as a possible market-regime control, not a stock-selection signal.
- Form 144 remains a more concrete event-panel candidate than the invalidated Phase 35 13D/G extraction: Phase 67 validated XML subject/filer roles and 21,634 issuer-event rows in a 2023Q2–2026Q3 census, but historical F80 membership, security-class/ticker mapping, acceptance-time availability and amendment-chain semantics still block alpha. ## 2026-11-05 — Research governance and source-gate supersession

### Research objective and model-selection policy
- Adopted `research/RESEARCH_OBJECTIVE_AND_MODEL_SELECTION_POLICY_2026-11-05.md`.

Do not attach the corrected issuer CIK/ticker to the parent date. Treat the 144/A as a new version of the same proposed-sale order, not a new sale. The corrected issuer identity is first known on the amendment filing date and can be used no earlier than the first regular NYSE session strictly after that date. Never backdate.

It resolved 1,442/1,446 parent links (99.723%); four unresolved parent accessions are explicitly retained. Parent form and CIK-pair concordance were 100% among resolved parents; no date inversions among comparable resolved links. The run failed only because the initial 56,167/609 gates were wrong; corrected baseline gates and tests are being rerun.
- Across 1,442 resolved pairs: 49 issuer CIK changes (24 with explicit identity-correction remarks), 96 issuer-name changes, 108 seller-name changes, and 767 proposed-sale tranche payload changes. Preserve the version transition; never backdate.
- SEC EDGAR filing detail pages / complete submission text expose ACCEPTANCE-DATETIME, but SEC says no timestamp records first public availability; availability is often 1–3 minutes after acceptance, can be longer, and is not guaranteed. Acceptance time is not first-public-receipt. Keep the next-regular-session PIT rule until a prospective first-seen ledger validates public availability. Official sources: https://www.sec.gov/about/webmaster-frequently-asked-questions ; https://www.sec.gov/submit-filings/filer-support-resources/how-do-i-guides/file-form-144-electronically.

No forward returns, alpha/OOS/bootstrap/LOO/holdout test; no signal admitted; F80/production unchanged.

## 2026-11-14 — CFTC historical publication-time feasibility correction

- Official CFTC Historical Viewable states that archive dates are report dates, not release dates. The About COT page says Futures Only reports are generally released Friday at 3:30 p.m. ET, but the agency's historical release-date list is available only for the latest 13 months. The public release schedule is tentative and holiday-adjusted.
- Historical release timing is not safely reconstructed as report-date + 3 days. CFTC's 2025 shutdown special announcement shows the 2025-09-30 report originally scheduled for 2025-10-03 was not published until 2025-11-19, with other reports released in catch-up order. A naive report-date clock would introduce material lookahead for that period.
- Therefore the Phase 384 prospective first-receipt ledger is not merely conservative; it is required for reproducible PIT beyond the CFTC's 13-month release-date history. Historical backfill stays ineligible. Phase 387 remains weekly until the TFF dataset itself demonstrates the announced semiweekly schedule.
- Official sources: CFTC Historical Viewable (https://www.cftc.gov/MarketReports/CommitmentsofTraders/HistoricalViewable/index.htm); About the COT Reports (https://www.cftc.gov/MarketReports/CommitmentsofTraders/AbouttheCOTReports/cot_about.html); Release Schedule (https://www.cftc.gov/MarketReports/CommitmentsofTraders/ReleaseSchedule/index.htm); Historical Special Announcements (https://www.cftc.gov/MarketReports/CommitmentsofTraders/HistoricalSpecialAnnouncements/index.htm); CFTC release 9316-26 (https://www.cftc.gov/PressRoom/PressReleases/9316-26).

## Phase 406 — Cboe Option Trades / OPRA schema and trade-condition feasibility (2026-11-14)

- PR [#83](https://github.com/jafelix81/NuevaEstrategia/pull/83) merged to main as `268c9ac009abc24551a4987702cab0a2dfc9c3a5`.
- Cboe Option Trades advertises OPRA-wide U.S. stock/ETF/index option trades from 2012-01, with millisecond Eastern trade timestamps, OPRA sequence numbers, trade conditions, exchange, trade price/size and prevailing NBBO; GTH and RTH are included. Optional trade-time IV/Greeks can default to zero when unavailable/invalid; underlying quotes can also default to zero. The file does not provide a separate NBBO quote-event timestamp or beneficial-owner/capacity identity.
- OPRA trade-type flags were implemented 2019-11-04. Published LiveVol mapping identifies condition 114 as SLAN (single-leg non-ISO price-improvement auction), 115–118 as other single-leg auction/cross/floor types, 119–133 as multi-leg/stock-option categories, and 40–43 as cancellation-related types. The official Cboe condition workbook must be reconciled before implementation.
- Economic interpretation is deliberately conservative: SLAN is a retail/wholesaler-auction proxy, not an investor identity. Published evidence finds short-horizon predictability in retail options flow more consistent with intermediary hedging/price pressure than informed trading. Separate auction/retail-like flow from complex package flow; do not label either “smart money.”
- The Cboe product page exposes a public sample link, but ZIP bytes were not obtained; no sample rows were inspected. Exact cancellation/bust linkage, historical inactive-underlying coverage, first-delivery/correction vintages, commercial rights and price remain open. No vendor contact, trial, purchase or data acquisition.
- Report: [Phase 406](https://github.com/jafelix81/NuevaEstrategia/blob/main/research/PHASE406_CBOE_OPTION_TRADES_OPRA_SCHEMA_AND_TRADE_CONDITION_AUDIT_2026-11-14.md); structured summary: [JSON](https://github.com/jafelix81/NuevaEstrategia/blob/main/research/PHASE406_CBOE_OPTION_TRADES_OPRA_SCHEMA_SUMMARY_2026-11-14.json).

## Phase 407 — Cboe SLIM evidence reconciliation and Option Trades PIT-delivery gate (2026-11-14)

- Phase 407 adds a material literature correction to Phase 406. Amaya, Garcia-Ares, Pearson & Vasquez (2025-04-07), *New Evidence on the Performance of Customer Options Trades* (Cboe-hosted; acknowledges Options Institute data-research grant support), finds that the BPS-style quote rule misclassifies 42.4% of Cboe SLIM trades / 42.6% of volume in its Cboe subset. 42.4% of SLIM trades / 42.6% of volume are closing trades; opening volume is 57.4%. Cboe customer capacity is >98% of SLIM volume, but the paper notes "customer" includes hedge funds, mutual funds and other institutional public customers; it is not a retail or beneficial-owner label.
- The paper reports average net lifecycle P&L of +$0.34m/day (t=0.29) for Jan 2020–Jun 2023 and +$2.07m/day (t=1.19) for Jan 2020–Jun 2021. Both are statistically insignificant; net P&L excludes commissions. It cannot match individual closing trades to their opening trades and assumes positions opened in the SLIM channel close in that channel. This is not an underlying-equity prediction test and does not establish alpha. Source: https://cdn.cboe.com/resources/education/research_publications/Retail_Profitability.pdf.
- Correct interpretation: SLAN/SLIM is an execution-mechanism proxy, not beneficial-owner identity. Do not label customer flow as retail/institutional informed flow. Separate estimated option-side direction, capacity, opening/closing and complex/package structure; quote-rule aggressor signing may be materially misclassified. The 2025 paper challenges blanket "retail loses" claims but does not demonstrate predictive equity alpha.
- Cboe Option Trades historical-start conflict: product page advertises Jan 2012; DataShop FAQ availability table lists Jan 2010. Keep start unresolved until actual date/symbol availability or written clarification. Product page: https://datashop.cboe.com/option-trades ; FAQ: https://datashop.cboe.com/faqs.
- Cboe FAQ says daily files are T+1 via SFTP, typically 00:05–03:00 ET, but delays can push delivery until after the next market open; files remain on SFTP for 30 days; intraday delivery requires separate contact. Therefore no assumption of next-open availability: capture actual first receipt, archive raw files/hashes and corrections, and if receipt is after 09:30 ET, defer use to the next eligible session. No account, vendor contact, sample purchase or data acquisition.
- The official condition workbook link was identified (https://datashop.cboe.com/documents/livevol_trade_condition_ids.xlsx) but XLSX bytes were not inspectable; the Option Trades public sample ZIP (https://datashop.cboe.com/download/sample/216) was likewise not inspectable due binary-content handling. No condition map or sample rows are claimed as verified.
- Existing Phase 351 TBT sample remains only a 3% one-day C1 sample (2025-03-28), with 17.86% blank OPEN_CLOSE, 0.45% incomplete contract identity, 69 unmapped SIDE rows and unresolved session/segment semantics. Its sampling rule is unverified; no population calibration or prevalence estimates. See draft PR #38 / branch phase351-cboe-tbt-public-sample-row-grain-audit.
- Report: research/PHASE407_CBOE_SLIM_EVIDENCE_AND_OPRA_PIT_DELIVERY_2026-11-14.md; structured summary: research/PHASE407_CBOE_SLIM_EVIDENCE_AND_OPRA_PIT_DELIVERY_SUMMARY_2026-11-14.json.

## Phase 426 — 2026-11-16: MOM120 exact F80 engine and exposure-matched shadow

- Re-tested the Phase 29 positive MOM120 candidate using the exact Phase 11 v4 frozen engine and its signal calendar, TOP20/PARTIAL50 state machine, position/gross caps and next-open execution. 335/335 tickers loaded; 2,573 cross-sectional snapshots; OOS 2024-01-02 to 2026-11-13; nine OOS 80-session signal refreshes.
- Raw C20 results favored MOM120, but its average gross exposure was higher (68.52% vs 63.30%), so the raw comparison was not accepted as exposure-controlled.
- Ran a separate shadow simulation with both rankings normalized at every daily open to the same 59.8667% gross target, fixed from frozen LTM C20 training-period average exposure (2016–2023). Same 10/20/50 bps per-side costs were applied to selection and gross-normalization trades.
- At C20, matched-exposure MOM120: 2.126x OOS multiple, 1.056 Sharpe, -27.20% max drawdown; frozen LTM: 1.474x, 0.624 Sharpe, -29.39% max drawdown. Average close gross exposure was 59.864% vs 59.853%.
- Paired MOM120-minus-LTM excess: 5.169 bps/day; HAC lag-80 t=3.79; block-bootstrap 95% CI remained positive for 20/40/80/120/160-session blocks; daily LOO positive 100%; eight of ten rebalance cycles positive and cycle LOO positive for every omission. Cost sensitivity remained positive at 10/20/50 bps per side.
- MOM120 is highly redundant with LTM (mean cross-sectional Spearman 0.948; TOP20 overlap 15.78/20). It is a momentum reweighting, not a new smart-money source.
- Decision: PROMISING_HISTORICAL_DIAGNOSTIC_REQUIRES_PROSPECTIVE_CONFIRMATION. This is not a fresh holdout: 2024+ was already examined in Phase 29/30, the 335-name universe is not PIT/survivorship-free, and only nine OOS rebalance refreshes are available. Phase 29 validation was weak.
- No baseline, holdout, universe, parameters, capital, production or exposure policy was changed. F80_TOP20_PARTIAL50_BASELINE_C20 remains frozen.
- Next: prospective shadow tracking, point-in-time universe reconstruction, and a MOM120 residual/conditional test versus LTM before any promotion.
- Final Actions run: [43789923762](https://github.com/jafelix81/NuevaEstrategia/actions/runs/43789923762); artifact [14191029663](https://github.com/jafelix81/NuevaEstrategia/actions/runs/43789923762/artifacts/14191029663); report [Phase 426](research/PHASE426_MOM120_FROZEN_F80_EXACT_ENGINE_AND_EXPOSURE_MATCHED_SHADOW_2026-11-16.md).

## Phase 430 — options signal identification and borrow-fee confounding (2026-11-16)

- Literature update: Pan & Poteshman (2006) find predictive content in buyer-initiated option volume that opens new positions; generic volume/put-call ratios do not reproduce that construct. Muravyev, Pearson & Pollet (2025, JFE 172, 104153) find IV spread/skew return predictability is substantially explained by stock-borrow fees; excluding high-fee stocks reduces predictability by at least two-thirds and fee-adjusted portfolio abnormal returns are insignificant. Do not treat raw IV spread/skew as Smart Money alpha; require point-in-time borrow-fee control or demote to friction/context features. FINRA short interest/short volume are not borrow-fee substitutes.
- Cboe Open-Close remains the leading conditional prospective source because it distinguishes capacity and opening/closing position-side flow, but its buy/sell fields are not aggressor flags, capacity is not beneficial-owner identity, and four Cboe venues are not all OPRA. ThetaData trade_quote provides a separate prior-NBBO aggressor estimate but lacks capacity/open-close. Do not collapse these measurements or call either institutional flow.
- No sample, license, historical PIT, coverage gate, return/alpha/OOS/LOO/holdout test or signal admission. F80, universe, parameters, capital, exposure and production remain unchanged.
- Report: [Phase 430](research/PHASE430_OPTIONS_SIGNAL_IDENTIFICATION_BORROW_FEE_CONFOUNDING_2026-11-16.md).
- Operational preflight at 2026-11-16 05:35 UTC: latest 100 Actions runs 62 success / 37 failure / 1 cancelled (7,666 total reported); zero active/queued/waiting/requested. Inspected 264 branches, 56 open PRs and 64 workflows. ## Phase 431 — SEC Form 4 AFF10B5ONE schema and semantics audit (2026-11-16)

- Reused immutable Phase 39 actual-file artifact 12781850078 from run 40606706518 (outer ZIP SHA-256 203d7b1d2e29c999da318456af72e621cb30ef9a470d8f1610d088f9bebecb66); no new SEC request.
- Actual 2012Q1 SUBMISSION.tsv has no AFF10B5ONE field across 63,678 Form 4/4A filings; classify as unknown, never unchecked. Actual 2026Q3 SUBMISSION.tsv has AFF10B5ONE (also present in archive metadata and HTML readme), but NONDERIV_TRANS.tsv does not. The SEC-hosted PDF readme table omits the field, so vintage-specific actual TSV + archive metadata take precedence.
- 2026Q3 Form 4/4A: 34,148 filings; 5,035 checked (14.74%) and 29,113 unchecked. Raw encodings include 1/true and 0/false. AFF10B5ONE is accession/filing-level, not transaction-row-level. 1,608 checked filings with transaction rows have multiple distinct transaction codes; 1,573 include S plus another code and two include P plus another code. Do not blindly propagate the flag to every row; checked accessions are conservatively plan-associated/contaminated unless original filing/footnotes allow adjudication.
- Form 4/4A P-code sample: 5,183 rows / 3,211 accessions, 82 checked accessions (2.55%); P means open-market **or private** purchase. S-code: 25,430 rows / 9,837 accessions, 4,777 checked (48.55%). These are filing-level associations, not row-level labels. Form 5 P/S rows are excluded from the Form 4 feature counts.
- SEC final rule: effective 2023-02-27; amended Forms 4/5 compliance from 2023-04-01. SEC C&DI 135.04 says the checkbox does not apply to plans adopted before the amendments' effective date. Unchecked does not mean discretionary; checked says intended to satisfy amended Rule 10b5-1(c), not legal validation of the affirmative defense. Quarterly bulk data remain a historical aid; use accession-level EDGAR acceptance time for PIT.
- Decision: schema/semantics gate PASS; Form 4 still NOT admitted for alpha because legacy identity remains 53/96 (55.21%), below the 95% full-universe gate. Next: inspect 2023Q2/Q3 actual vintages and continuity, footnote/Item 408 plan context, and continue effective-dated issuer/security identity. No return/alpha/OOS/LOO/holdout test.
- Report: research/PHASE431_FORM4_AFF10B5ONE_SCHEMA_AND_SEMANTICS_AUDIT_2026-11-16.md; reproducible script/test and compact sample JSON are adjacent.
- PR #94's hosted regression run [43816028156](https://github.com/jafelix81/NuevaEstrategia/actions/runs/43816028156) completed successfully: compile passed, 5/5 offline tests passed. No SEC request, data purchase or production change.

## Phase 432 — Item 408 trading-arrangement PIT feasibility (2026-11-16)

- New candidate source: SEC Regulation S-K Item 408(a) quarterly disclosure of director/officer Rule 10b5-1 and non-Rule 10b5-1 trading arrangements. Decision: structured-source and schema feasibility PASS; PIT is conditional on EDGAR acceptance time; historical depth limited to staggered 2023 starts; universe/person coverage unmeasured; NOT admitted for alpha.
- SEC final rule effective 2023-02-27. Form 4/5 checkbox compliance applies to filings on/after 2023-04-01. Item 408(a) periodic-report compliance starts with first full fiscal period beginning on/after 2023-04-01 for non-SRCs and 2023-10-01 for SRCs. Calendar-year first expected quarters: 2023 Q2 and 2023 Q4 respectively. Item 408(a) covers domestic issuer directors/officers only, not all Section 16 reporting persons; FPIs and registered investment companies are outside this Reg S-K requirement.
- ECD taxonomy provides IndividualAxis and TradingArrAxis plus standard facts for person name/title, Rule/non-Rule adoption/termination, adoption/termination dates, duration/expiration, aggregate shares and material-terms text block. Parser must preserve both dimensions, contextRef, units and hidden iXBRL facts. Natural expiration/completion is not a reportable termination (SEC C&DI 133A.01); absent termination is not evidence a plan remains active.
- Real SEC sample: NOW Inc. 2023 Q3 10-Q accession 0001373715-23-000419 accepted 2023-10-25 19:20:50Z. It reports CEO William R. McDermott's Rule 10b5-1 plan adopted 2023-08-31 (30,489 shares plus net shares from 9,986 gross shares; expiration 2025-02-18) and director Paul E. Chamberlain's plan adopted 2023-08-21 (540 shares; expiration 2024-09-03). Acceptance preceded the 90-day dates by 35 and 25 days respectively. This is a real ex-ante example, not evidence all disclosures precede first plan trades; compare each filing acceptance against the plan's rule-based earliest eligible date.
- Use EDGAR acceptance timestamp as knowledge time, never plan adoption date or period end. Version 10-Q/A amendments as new events. Item 408 has no individual owner CIK; link names to Form 4 owners only with auditable match grades. Aggregate shares are plan capacity, not actual execution; do not sum multiple contexts/plan dimensions blindly. Distinguish Rule and non-Rule plans.
- Historical coverage is short (roughly 3–3.5 years through 2026 Q3) and event-sparse; SEC Form 345 bulk page says 2023–2025 datasets were updated in July 2025 to include AFF10B5ONE, but 2023Q2/Q3 ZIP bytes were not independently hash-verified in this phase. No alpha/OOS/LOO/holdout test or signal admission. Next: parser + stratified filing census, PIT issuer/person identity coverage audit, prospective raw ledger.
- Report: [Phase 432](research/PHASE432_ITEM408_TRADING_ARRANGEMENTS_PIT_FEASIBILITY_2026-11-16.md).
- Operational preflight 2026-11-16 ~08:08 UTC: latest 100 Actions runs 62 success / 37 failure / 1 cancelled (7,668 total reported), zero active/queued. ## Phase 433 — Item 408 parser scaffold and EDGAR acceptance-clock correction (2026-11-16)

- Corrected Phase 432's sample: CIK 0001373715 is ServiceNow, Inc., not "NOW Inc."; its 2023 Q3 Form 10-Q Item 5 lists three plans (Gina Mastantuono, William R. McDermott and Paul E. Chamberlain), not two. The omitted CFO plan is 2023-07-28, 317 shares plus net shares from 8,019 gross vesting shares, stated expiration 2024-05-24. CEO: 2023-08-31, 30,489 plus net shares from 9,986 gross vesting shares, expiration 2025-02-18. Director: 2023-08-21, 540 shares, expiration 2024-09-03. These are plan terms/capacity, not executions.
- Material PIT correction: SEC SGML header `<ACCEPTANCE-DATETIME>20231025192050` is Eastern wall-clock, not 19:20:50Z. SEC PDS specification labels acceptance time EST; 17 CFR 232.12–.13 specifies EST or EDT whichever is currently in effect. On 2023-10-25 New York was EDT (UTC−04:00), so the converted instant is 2023-10-25T23:20:50Z. The official 10-Q filing date is 2023-10-26 because acceptance occurred after the 5:30 p.m. Eastern cutoff. Earlier Phase 432 wording labelled the raw clock UTC and was four hours too early.
- Additional no-lookahead refinement: SEC says there is no timestamp for when filing content first becomes available on sec.gov; documents are often available 1–3 minutes after acceptance, sometimes materially later. Treat acceptance as an EDGAR event, not guaranteed external availability. For historical daily tests, use next regular trading session after Eastern acceptance date unless a captured PDS/first-seen receipt proves earlier availability. Preserve acceptance and official filing date separately.
- Added `research/phase433_item408_ixbrl_parser.py`: standard-library Inline XBRL parser preserving IndividualAxis/TradingArrAxis, contexts, hidden facts, continuation chains, nil/unresolved states, numeric scale and duplicate fact grain; no plan-status, execution, intent or aggregation inference. Added 12 synthetic regression tests in `research/test_phase433_item408_ixbrl_parser.py`; all 12 passed locally. The fixture is synthetic, not raw ServiceNow filing bytes; real-byte integration/hash remains pending.
- Report: `research/PHASE433_ITEM408_PARSER_AND_ACCEPTANCE_CLOCK_CORRECTION_2026-11-16.md`. PR-only regression workflow: `.github/workflows/phase433_item408_ixbrl_parser.yml`.
- Admission: parser scaffold PASS on synthetic fixtures only; real raw-file integration PENDING; exact public dissemination timestamp unavailable in archive; history limited to staggered 2023 start; issuer/security coverage unmeasured; person-to-Form-4 identity unresolved; alpha/OOS NOT AUTHORIZED. The SEC contact secret `SEC_USER_AGENT` remains a prerequisite for automated raw-file retrieval; no secret was read or changed.
- No alpha, return, OOS, bootstrap, LOO or holdout test; no new source/signal admitted; no data purchased. `F80_TOP20_PARTIAL50_BASELINE_C20`, holdout, universe, parameters, capital, exposure and production remain frozen.

### Phase 433 addendum — SEC C&DI 120.29 cooling-off dates (2026-11-16)

- Checked SEC C&DI Question 120.29. The filing date of the relevant 10-Q/10-K does not count as the first business day; if filed Monday, trading may commence Thursday absent holidays. For ServiceNow's Thursday 2023-10-26 10-Q filing, the two following business days were Friday 2023-10-27 and Monday 2023-10-30, so the first potential trading date after that limb expired was Tuesday 2023-10-31.
- Assuming no later plan modification or other restriction, earliest Rule 10b5-1 cooling-off dates are: Gina Mastantuono 2023-10-31 (later than her 90-day date 2023-10-26); William R. McDermott 2023-11-29 (90-day limb dominates); Paul E. Chamberlain 2023-11-20 (90-day date 2023-11-19 fell on Sunday; next regular session). Conservative Item 408 availability begins no earlier than the 2023-10-26 session: five calendar days before Gina's potential first trade, 34 before McDermott's and 25 before Chamberlain's. This is regulatory timing only, not evidence of execution or plan continuity.
- Updated report: `research/PHASE433_ITEM408_PARSER_AND_ACCEPTANCE_CLOCK_CORRECTION_2026-11-16.md`. Source: SEC Exchange Act Rules C&DI 120.29, https://www.sec.gov/rules-regulations/staff-guidance/corporation-finance-interpretations/exchange-act-rules.

 
## Phase 434 — Item 408 / Form 4 cross-source temporal reconciliation (2026-11-16)

- Hand-audited ServiceNow 2023 Q3 Item 408 against selected Form 4 XML records for Gina Mastantuono, William R. McDermott and Paul E. Chamberlain. Casebook, analyzer and tests: research/PHASE434_ITEM408_FORM4_CASES_2026-11-16.json, research/phase434_item408_form4_temporal_reconciliation.py, research/test_phase434_item408_form4_temporal_reconciliation.py; report: research/PHASE434_ITEM408_FORM4_CROSS_SOURCE_TEMPORAL_RECONCILIATION_2026-11-16.md.
- Gina: Form 4 accession 0001373715-23-000411 has AFF10B5ONE=1 and two S-coded, plan-footnoted disposals dated 2023-10-09 (193 shares each; 386 total) tied to a plan dated 2023-07-28. The Item 408 10-Q was filed 2023-10-26. Under SEC C&DI 120.29 date-only calculation, the two-business-day limb expires 2023-10-30 and the first subsequent weekday session is 2023-10-31. The 2023-10-09 transactions precede that calculated threshold. Flag as an apparent chronology inconsistency only, not a legal conclusion; the cooling-off condition governs the affirmative defense, not a blanket ban on trading. A later 922-share S-coded Form 4 disposal is dated 2023-11-01.
- William: Item 408 adoption 2023-08-31; first observed plan-footnoted S-coded Form 4 event in this sample 2024-02-20, after the 2023-11-30 conservative first session. Item 408 preceded this observed event by 117 calendar days.
- Paul: Item 408 adoption 2023-08-21 vs Form 4 footnote adoption 2023-08-31, a 10-day discrepancy. The 135-share S-coded Form 4 transaction dated 2023-11-30 is after either calculated threshold.
- Sample result: 3 plans, 1 adoption-date mismatch, 1 plan with a selected S-coded transaction before the Item 408-based calculated threshold, and 2 plans where Item 408 filing preceded the first observed S-coded transaction. These are descriptive counts from one issuer, not a population estimate.
- Method rule: keep AFF10B5ONE as a form-level filer flag; count plan-linked transactions only when row-level footnotes explicitly tie them to the plan; do not infer open-market venue, plan continuity, execution of disclosed capacity, or legal compliance. Use filing/first-seen availability, never plan adoption date, for PIT.
- Item 408 remains a conditional potential-supply source. Universe coverage, full lifecycle, identity coverage across the 335-stock universe and incremental value versus LTM/F80 remain unaudited. No alpha/OOS/LOO/holdout test; no source admission.
- No production, trading parameter, universe, capital or exposure changes. F80_TOP20_PARTIAL50_BASELINE_C20 remains frozen.

## Phase 436 — Item 408 cross-issuer schema and plan-semantics stress audit — 2026-11-16

- Added a purposive six-issuer SEC sample: Juniper Networks (JNPR), Dexcom (DXCM), CarGurus (CARG), Powell Industries (POWL), Celanese (CE), and General Mills (GIS). This is a schema/semantics stress sample, not a random sample, universe coverage estimate, or alpha sample.
- The casebook contains 12 plan actions: 11 adoptions and one termination. Direction classes: 9 sell, 1 buy, 1 exercise-and-sell, and 1 unresolved. One action is a $45,000 plan termination, not a share count. No amounts are summed across USD/shares, buy/sell, gross/net, conditional awards or unresolved direction.
- Juniper's 188,669-share row is under a “purchased or sold” heading and does not resolve direction; it remains unknown. Celanese's 4,000-share plan is explicitly in the purchased column. General Mills' 57,879-share plan is tied to stock-option exercise and sale. CarGurus includes both new adoptions and a prior plan termination, and gross future-award amounts before withholding. Dexcom has future vesting/tax/PSU/ESPP/limit-order caveats and a trust relationship. Powell's filing supplies explicit future plan-window start dates.
- Code review: the current Phase 433 ECD concept allowlist contains aggregate-available and text-block concepts but no dedicated buy/sell direction concept. Direction must be recovered from narrative/table semantics or left unknown; the aggregate-available fact alone is not a sale signal.
- First eligible sessions are conservative next-regular-session boundaries after the filing date. SEC filing-index acceptance timestamps are preserved in Eastern time but are not certified first-public receipt.
- Decision: Item 408 remains a conditional plan-activity / potential-supply source, not an admitted sell signal. The naive “shares in 10b5-1 plan = future selling” feature is rejected because it would misclassify purchases, exercise-and-sell awards, gross/conditional quantities, lifecycle terminations and unknown direction.
- No stock-return, price-impact, alpha, incremental LTM/F80, OOS, bootstrap, LOO or holdout test was run. No production or frozen baseline changes.
- Added the Phase 436 casebook, analyzer, tests and report. Extended the existing Phase 433 regression workflow; no new workflow or live capture was created.

### Phase 438 — Item 408 post-amendment information-content and disclosure-lag audit — 2026-11-16

- New full-text 2025 working-paper evidence (Kim, Kim & Rajgopal) finds 10b5-1 plan sales made within 90 days of adoption fell from 31.1% to 1.7% after the 2022 amendments; plan terminations disclosed in 10-Q/K filings averaged 82.4 days after the actual termination (median 83; n=311, June 2023–May 2025). Reported positive abnormal returns are measured around the termination event, not necessarily from public disclosure; only two sample terminations were promptly disclosed on 8-K. Treat the economic termination date as latent/non-tradable when public disclosure is delayed.
- 2026 IREF article by Yun Ma (DOI 10.1016/j.iref.2026.105650; abstract only accessible) reports reduced predictive content of preplanned trades after the amendments and non-preplanned signals confined to the short term. Older pre-amendment 10b5-1 evidence is regime-dependent and must not be pooled mechanically with post-reform data.
- CarGurus casebook cross-check: Quinn plan terminated 2024-03-04 and was disclosed in the 2024-05-09 10-Q, a 66-calendar-day lag. This is one case, not a population estimate.
- Item 408 is downgraded to a low-priority plan-lifecycle/context source; no directional signal is admitted. Any future test must use first public EDGAR acceptance, compare event-date diagnostics with post-disclosure returns, and test incremental value vs frozen LTM/F80 under chronological OOS and identical costs/exposure.

## Phase 445 — FINRA short-interest zero-vs-missing semantics re-audit (2026-11-17)

- Decision: **reopen for data-semantics / effective-coverage reconstruction only; no alpha re-admission**.
- Phase 37's 98/335 figure was positive-row incidence in the Phase 31 artifact, not necessarily missing-data coverage. FINRA Regulatory Notice 16-32 explicitly says firms need not submit a zero position when a previously reported issue has no short position in the current cycle. A no-row observation may therefore be a structural zero only after the entire settlement snapshot is proven complete, the security is proven active/in-scope on that date, and the effective-dated identity is resolved. Failed/partial snapshots and unresolved/inactive/out-of-scope symbols remain unknown, never zero.
- Phase 31 artifact facts remain: 9,420 consolidated-SI rows, 98 unique symbols with rows, 84–96 symbols with positive rows per audited settlement, dates 2022-06-15 to 2026-09-30. These counts measure positive-position incidence, not full effective coverage. Phase 31's unstable horizon directions and bootstrap intervals including zero remain non-promotable; this phase did not run a return test.
- FINRA's January 2023 release notes state five years of history and all-exchange short-interest data from June 2022. FINRA API docs say the consolidated dataset is available by 4:40 p.m. ET on the official publication date. The current calendar remains twice monthly; Nov 13 settlement is due Nov 17 at 6 p.m. ET and publishes Nov 24. SEC marks SR-FINRA-2026-012 withdrawn on Aug 5, 2026; proposed weekly cadence, arranged-financing additions and deleted-symbol final reports are not in force.
- Preserve `revisionFlag`, `stockSplitFlag`, raw current/previous short quantities, complete-snapshot status, active security identity, official publication clock and prospective first-seen clock. Do not treat FINRA reported short interest as total economic short exposure or direct institutional identity.
- Required next gate: reconstruct full per-settlement snapshots with explicit states (positive row / structural zero / unknown), then recompute effective 335-name coverage using effective-dated security identity. Only if that gate passes may a new chronological diagnostic be designed against frozen LTM/F80 with identical exposure/costs, bootstrap/LOO and return-chasing controls.
- Report: [Phase 445](research/PHASE445_FINRA_SHORT_INTEREST_ZERO_VS_MISSING_SEMANTICS_AUDIT_2026-11-17.md) · [machine-readable summary](research/PHASE445_FINRA_SHORT_INTEREST_ZERO_VS_MISSING_SUMMARY_2026-11-17.json).

## Phase 446 — Phase 31 FINRA short-interest artifact truncation audit (2026-11-17)

- Decision: **Phase 31 short-interest panel is killed as retrieved; FINRA source remains conditional on complete asynchronous re-ingestion.**

- `si_download_audit.csv`: 106 settlement requests; 102 successes and 4 errors. **All 102 successful requests returned exactly 5,000 rows**, the documented FINRA synchronous API ceiling. The audit saved no `Record-Total`, `Record-Offset`, `Record-Limit`, async request ID or pagination-complete metadata. None of the 102 snapshots is proven complete.
- `short_interest_features.csv`: 9,420 filtered rows, 102 settlement dates (2022-06-15 to 2026-09-30), 98 unique observed tickers. All 98 begin A–D (34 A, 19 B, 32 C, 13 D); no E–Z. All retained current short quantities are positive. This is strongly consistent with prefix truncation, although raw API response order was not preserved. **98 is only the observed symbol count in a capped subset, not full positive-position incidence and not effective 335-name coverage.** No absent symbol may be zero-filled from this artifact.
- Four failed settlements: 2024-01-15, 2024-03-29, 2026-10-15 and 2026-10-30. The last is beyond Phase 31's 2026-10-23 OOS end.
- FINRA API docs state 5,000 synchronous and 100,000 asynchronous record ceilings and document response headers such as Record-Total/Offset/Limit. Next re-ingestion must use async requests (or deterministic partitions), retain request IDs/timestamps/headers, and reconcile each settlement to its source total before any structural-zero inference.
- Phase 445's zero-vs-missing semantics remain valid in principle, but its structural-zero rule cannot be applied to the Phase 31 artifact because snapshot completeness was never established. Phase 37's kill of the existing Phase 31 panel remains valid and is strengthened; the source itself is conditional on complete retrieval.
- Local reproducible audit script plus five synthetic tests passed 5/5: [report](research/PHASE446_FINRA_SHORT_INTEREST_ARTIFACT_TRUNCATION_AUDIT_2026-11-17.md) · [summary](research/PHASE446_FINRA_SHORT_INTEREST_ARTIFACT_TRUNCATION_SUMMARY_2026-11-17.json) · [auditor](research/phase446_finra_si_artifact_audit.py) · [tests](research/test_phase446_finra_si_artifact_audit.py).

No live FINRA request was made.

The client also validates the pre-signed HTTPS AWS S3 host, rejects an invalid poll interval before submission, and writes output with restrictive local permissions. Added an isolated offline CI workflow; no live secret is exposed to the job.

## Phase 463 — Independent SEC ownership lake audit (2026-11-17)

- Reviewed external MIT implementation `austin-starks/sec-ownership-disclosures` (0.4.1) and its parser, tests, date utilities, crosswalk and methodology. Retain only as a conditional secondary parser/crosswalk audit; no external binary snapshot was downloaded or pinned.
- The parser stores one transaction leg per accession and one filing row per reporting owner. This avoids duplication but supplies no transaction-to-owner link; multi-owner transaction rows remain quarantined.
- SEC code P means open-market **or private** purchase. P/A without footnote/filing evidence of an open-market execution remains unresolved; the external parser does not publish the FOOTNOTES table in its transaction rows.
- Its Form 4 `resolvedTicker` is based on filer-entered issuer ticker text and first-class parsing, not an effective-dated security-class master. Its 13F helper supports date spans, but the public example passes undated pairs from one N-PORT quarter; historical mapping is not admitted.
- Quarterly `availableAt` is filing-date end-of-day New York: conservative next-session only, not intraday. 13F is a lagged holdings snapshot, not trade flow.
- Exact snapshot manifest/hash and source commit remain unpinned; GitHub README and Hugging Face card report different snapshot vintages/counts. No source or signal admitted.
- Phase 463 report: [external SEC ownership lake audit](research/PHASE463_SEC_OWNERSHIP_LAKE_EXTERNAL_SOURCE_AUDIT_2026-11-17.md) · [summary](research/PHASE463_SEC_OWNERSHIP_LAKE_EXTERNAL_SOURCE_SUMMARY_2026-11-17.json).

## Phase 467 — FINRA Reg SHO flow-only and Phase 31 PIT label audit (2026-11-18)

- Independently downloaded and inspected existing GitHub artifact 12588045711 from Phase 31 run 40164323397; ZIP SHA-256 `91f7683cf984cb2b00a6bd772608c3243ac9a56717930c6fd2092696e092a266`. The flow panel contains 362,727 rows, 334 symbols, and 1,104/1,104 XNYS sessions from 2022-06-01 to 2026-10-23; daily cross-section is 321–333 (median 330), and 313/334 symbols have at least 1,100 observations. This reopens **flow-only data feasibility** as a conditional candidate, not alpha.
- Material PIT correction: Phase 31 uses same-day flow rows `Date <= signal_date` but computes forward returns from `close[signal_date]`. FINRA says the daily file is posted after regular trading hours. The label therefore starts before the flow observation is available. Phase 31 return/IC/spread results are diagnostic only and invalid as strict-PIT evidence; do not use for alpha claims or feature selection.
- FINRA Daily Short Sale Volume is off-exchange FINRA-facility short-marked volume, not consolidated market volume, net short flow, persistent short interest, or institutional identity. FINRA explicitly distinguishes it from short-interest positions. Historical exact first-seen times are not present in the daily files; prospective capture or explicitly lagged sensitivity is required.
- The joint Phase 31 flow × short-interest panel remains non-admissible: the old SI snapshots were capped/incomplete. No flow-only return test, OOS, bootstrap, LOO, signal admission, or production change was performed.
- Report: [Phase 467](research/PHASE467_FINRA_REGSHO_FLOW_ONLY_AND_PHASE31_PIT_LABEL_AUDIT_2026-11-18.md) · [summary](research/PHASE467_FINRA_REGSHO_FLOW_ONLY_AND_PHASE31_PIT_LABEL_SUMMARY_2026-11-18.json).

- Phase 469's same-New-York-date-only eligibility rule would permanently classify a file as bootstrap if two scheduled polls had recorded HTTP 403/404 and the valid file first arrived on a later day. This discards a genuinely prospective local first receipt even though the collector has evidence it did not receive the file earlier.
- Phase 470 adds `late_first_seen` only when the append-only poll ledger contains an earlier scheduled `not_available` poll for the exact trade date, with its poll end before the current poll start and after 16:00 ET. Without that evidence, old-date first captures remain bootstrap. Manual polls, invalid files and revisions cannot qualify.
- Phase 468 now accepts late first-seen events but still requires receipt strictly after the original trade-date close; entry is the first XNYS open strictly after the actual later receipt. Signal age is retained, and every observation remains `signal_eligible=false`.
- This is collector-specific first-receipt evidence, not a claim about the exact public release time. No historical availability is imputed and no backdating is permitted.
- SEC Phase 403 known production run [44179257367](https://github.com/jafelix81/NuevaEstrategia/actions/runs/44179257367) completed both jobs successfully and produced artifact [14371301851](https://github.com/jafelix81/NuevaEstrategia/actions/runs/44179257367/artifacts/14371301851), but it predates Phase 465 pagination. CFTC Phase 384 known bootstrap run [43525140200](https://github.com/jafelix81/NuevaEstrategia/actions/runs/43525140200) completed successfully and produced artifact [14084802705](https://github.com/jafelix81/NuevaEstrategia/actions/runs/43525140200/artifacts/14084802705); it is not a scheduled prospective capture. The connected GitHub interface does not expose a complete current workflow-run listing or administrative enabled state, so no claim is made about current global active runs or workflow enablement.
- Official FINRA semantics remain restrictive: daily short-sale files include only publicly disseminated transactions, are not consolidated with exchange data, and do not represent short-interest positions. FINRA also describes offsetting customer purchases that can be absent from the public file. Sources: https://www.finra.org/rules-guidance/notices/information-notice-051019 and https://www.finra.org/sites/default/files/2021-06/Regulatory-Notice-21-19.pdf.
- No raw market data, credentials, backtest, alpha/OOS/bootstrap/LOO or signal admission. `F80_TOP20_PARTIAL50_BASELINE_C20` remains frozen.
